Are without intrinsic nature;
Therefore you are called the Omniscient One.

The god's son Armor of Power said:

What phenomena are and what their nature is,
And the phenomenon by which they are expressed,
You have realized just as it truly is;
Therefore you have no attachment in the three times.

The god's son Desiring Power said:

Through wrong imagining, affliction
Of four kinds comes to arise;
You know that these are born of karma;
Therefore you have no equal.

The god's son Knowing Power said:

The antidotes to the afflictions and to karma,
Purification,
And the teaching as well, you thoroughly know;
Therefore you have no equal.

The god's son Light of Power said:

Meditative concentrations, liberations, emergence,
Attainments and samādhis,
Affliction and purification:
You have taught that these are mere names.

The god's son Arisen from Power said:

"Meditative concentration, liberation, purification,
meditative attainment and samādhi
all arise from conscientiousness,
and your wisdom engages with them."

The god's son Unfolding Power said:
"Hero, your knowledge
engages with concentration and the rest,
with emergence and with purification,
and like the wind it has no attachment."

The god's son Skilled in the Past said:
"For countless tens of millions of eons,
when you dwelt in this existence long ago,
you were never mixed with the faults of existence;
therefore you have no equal."

The god's son Abiding in the Past said:
"For countless tens of millions of eons
you sought immeasurable awakening,
and the Guide practiced it completely;
therefore you are the Hero."

The god's son Realizing the Past said:
"In the past you gave various gifts
and made requests when those of supreme mind taught;
therefore that Guide has surely come forth
fully endowed with manifold eloquence."

The god's son Purified in the Past said:
"When formerly you practiced in saṃsāra,
skilled in method among all the ways,
you beheld the lamps of the world;
therefore you are skilled in method."

The god's son Who Has Completed the Past said:
"In the past the Hero practiced long
under many Dharma kings;
now, certain to be omniscient,
I bow to you, the wise one among beings."

The god's son Perfect in Past Conduct said:
"In order to seek supreme awakening,
for countless tens of millions of eons,
with infinite tens of millions of buddhas
you planted the seed."

The god's son Certain of Past Conduct said:
"Having investigated for tens of millions of eons,
you know with certainty that all phenomena
are without a sentient being;
the Victor says they are mere coming together."

The god's son Reflecting on the Past said:
"For countless tens of millions of eons
the Guide practiced so as to know fully
what is possible and what is impossible;
therefore he is not deluded about the world."

The god's son Investigating the Past said:
"Relying on what is possible and impossible,
you investigated actions and their causes;
thus you know elements, faculties and inclinations,
the concentrations, and also the past."

The god's son Thoroughly Examining the Past said:
"Having thoroughly examined your past,
the Great Hero unfolded
the ten wisdoms of the powers;
with these he beholds this world."

The god's son Domain of the Past said:
"Hero, because you know
the past conduct of all beings
and the courses of virtue and nonvirtue,
you bring healing to the world."

The god's son Discerning Past Conduct said:
"The Sage fully knows
the past conduct of beings of old,
and knows their elements and inclinations;
therefore he brings healing to beings."

The god's son Mighty in Inclination said:
"Even if one examines the past
for infinite tens of millions of eons,
your mind holds no attachment;
therefore I have faith in the Protector."

The god's son Devoted to Wisdom said:
"Even if one contemplates past existences
for countless tens of millions of eons,
there is no one able to reach the far shore
of the qualities of the Lord of the two-legged."

The god's son Skilled in the Future said:
"The various beings, endowed with karma,
of these worlds of the ten directions,
who die, pass on and are born again,
you behold with the eye of a buddha."

The god's son Delivered from the Future said:
"By what cause one performs what action
and so goes into the six streams of existence,
all this the Victor knows;
therefore he is called the Omniscient One."

The god's son Directly Knowing Death and Birth said:
"By which element which being
is imbued and performs actions,
the habitual tendencies, the actions and the beings,
you know without wavering."

The god's son Aware of Death and Birth said:
"What kind of spiritual friend one relies on,
what action one forms,
and to which destiny one thereby goes,
Sage, that too you know."

The god's son Skilled in Purpose said:
"Whatever is to be done by that action,
its purpose you fully know;
for whose sake the action was done,
that too you fully know without remainder."

The god's son Skilled in Things said:
"Whatever things are taken as objects
and whatever action is formed,
the Omniscient One knows;
that is the domain of the Buddha."

The god's son Knower of Things said:
"How the things that beings cleverly conceive
abide, and the action
by which they come to be established,
you directly know."

The god's son Skilled in Cognition said:
"By which cognition a thing
has been conceived, you fully know,
and how one has become attached to it,
Sage, you know perfectly."

The god's son Skilled in Formation said:
"By which attachment, by which affliction
those actions have been formed,
those afflictions you truly know;
therefore you are my protector."

The god's son Skilled in Destinies said:
"To which destiny one goes by which action,
and what obscuration one meets
so that one does not attain the fruit,
all this, One with Eyes, you know."

The god's son Skilled in Objects of Focus said:
"For those born into the destinies,
the object through which their actions ripen
you fully know,
and your intelligence engages with it."

The god's son Skilled in Habitual Tendencies said:
"Of the evil deeds that people have done
in one destiny after another,
and likewise of their virtuous deeds,
you fully know the habitual tendencies."

The god's son Skilled in Karma said:
"Of all creatures in the destinies,
the three kinds of karma, old, new and later,
you know;
about these ordinary beings are confused."

The god's son Skilled in Maturation said:
"Small with great maturation,
great with small maturation,
or small, great or equal:
about these the non-Buddhists (tīrthikas) are deluded."

The god's son Skilled in Karma said:
"Whatever actions belong to this life,
whatever actions belong to the next birth,
and whatever belong to other times,
these, One with Eyes, you know."

The god's son Skilled in What Is Experienced in This Life said:
"The maturation of actions done in this very life,
which certain people come to experience
and about which ordinary beings are deluded,
you know."

The god's son Skilled in What Is Experienced After Rebirth said:
"Who has accumulated what action
that ripens after one is born,
of which the world cannot find the depth,

You of great wisdom know exactly that.

The god's son Skilled in What Is Experienced in Other Lifetimes said:
Karma that ripens at a later time,
After ten million eons have gone by,
And that comes forth at other times as well:
All this you know exactly as it is.

The god's son Skilled in What Is Experienced Unripened said:
Whatever actions have been done,
So long as they have not fully ripened,
For that long they yield no fruit;
This too the Supreme among humans knows.

The god's son Examining What Is Experienced Unripened said:
Endowed with whatever karma
This being comes to take birth:
The Undefeated knows it,
And, having examined it thoroughly, knows it fully.

The god's son Skilled in What Is Experienced Fully Ripened said:
How karma, once it has fully ripened,
Turns into a white result, you know.
Your wisdom penetrates it,
And you discern all these as empty.

The god's son Certain about What Is Experienced Fully Ripened said:
The Omniscient One fully knows
The heaviness and lightness of actions,
And comprehends the intentions of beings;
Therefore the Blessed One is my master.

The god's son Skilled in Heavy and Light said:
The karma by which sentient beings
Go to the lower realms,
Heavy and light alike, you see;
Knowing this, the Knower knows it fully.

The god's son Skilled in the Conduct of Beings said:
Taking full account of beings' conduct,
The heaviness and lightness of their karma
You, O Hero, fully know;
About this the non-Buddhists are confused.

The god's son Investigating the Conduct of Beings said:
Investigating the conduct of beings,
You teach the ripening of karma,
Just as the water in a cup and the water of the Ganges
Are told apart by salt.

The god's son Skilled in Karma said:
The supreme among humans teaches
The ripening for those in training and those beyond training,
And teaches how, once afflicted karma has been experienced,
Karma comes to be exhausted.

The god's son Touching the Wisdom of the Buddha said:
With the buddha eye you see, throughout the three realms,
The fools and the wise
Who rely on the fruits of karma.
Homage to you who see the subtle!

The god's son Exhausting Karma said:
You, the Guide within the worlds,
Your wisdom penetrates the death, transmigration,
And every birth of sentient beings.
Knowing this, Fearless One, I bow to you.

The god's son Engaging in the Nonreferential said:
Death and transmigration, birth and karma,
Destinies, and likewise beings,
And ripening are taught to be like a dream;
So the Guide, supreme among humans, knows.

The god's son Examining Afflictions said:
Just as a thirsty person in a dream
Drinks water that is perfectly cool,
So, supreme among humans, in you attachment,
Aversion and delusion are exhausted.

The god's son Free from Conceptualization said:
Attachment, aversion and delusion
Are born from conceptualization,
Like joyful play within a dream.
Supreme of speakers, I bow to you.

The god's son Pacifying Conceptualization said:
Through the complete pacifying of conceptualization
You fully teach the exhaustion of afflictions;
Like a cloudless sky within a dream,
Supreme of humans, you fully know.

The god's son Examining Defilements said:
Like a great rain falling in a dream,
So do the defilements arise.
Skilled in the arising of defilements,
Great Hero, you have taught this.

The god's son Examining Dreams said:
Like a great rain falling in a dream,
So do the defilements arise;
That defilements arise in those who err,
Great Hero, you have taught.

The god's son Examining Things as They Are said:
Just as a girl, in a dream,
Gives birth to a son who then dies,
And feels both joy and suffering,
So you see the beings of the world.

The god's son Realizing Dreams said:
Like being beheaded in a dream,
So you see the exhaustion of defilements;
This you teach to the wise.
Homage to the All-Seeing One!

The god's son Mind of Certain Examination said:
You who know all phenomena
See that, as in a dream, meeting with friends
And parting from them have no true existence,
And you satisfy beings according to their needs.

The god's son Wisdom Mind said:
When one hears you teach the sublime Dharma,
The mind becomes joyful;
Just as a dream is shown within a dream,
So the Victor gives his teaching.

The god's son Wholly Joyful Mind said:
Since you see all beings
As reflections appearing in a mirror,
In you, possessor of meditative concentration,
The world with its gods rejoices in mind.

The god's son Certain Intellect said:
O Sage, when they hear your sublime Dharma,
Which is like the sound of a lute,
Even the sons of the gods cast off their doubts
And bow down to the supreme Victor.

The god's son Satisfied by Illusion said:
When a magician displays illusions
That bewilder the childish,
The unskilled are confused by them;
You teach that things are like an illusion.

The god's son Contemplating Emanation said:
Just as one sees an illusion in a dream,
So you see the beings of the world.
Having heard this teaching of the Protector,
We have no doubt at all.

The god Released from Illusion said:

"Though a magician conjures an illusion,
Since he knows it to be an illusion,
He is not deluded by it.
In just this way do you see beings."

The god Following Thought said:

"Just as one who strives, in a dream,
Imagines the form of a buddha's body,
So conceptual thought gives rise to the world.
This, O Guide, is what you see."

The god Destroyer of Conceptualization said:

"Conceptualization is the cause of attachment;
Attachment is not produced by things.
From wrong thinking attachment arises.
This, O Guide, is what you see."

The god Endowed with Full Understanding said:

"The sound that arises in mountain ravines,
The echo, is not the sound of a sentient being.
In just this way do you see the world;
Therefore you have no attachment to the world."

The god Skilled in Teaching said:

"There is no sound whatsoever;
Sound is like an echo.
Yet, lord of men, you have taught,
And led many childish beings in."

The god Skilled in Diligence said:

"Though the meaning of phenomena is one thing,
The childish imagine it as another.
With diligence one should come to know it from the Dharma;
In this my doubts are cut away."

The god Follower of the Meaning said:

"Whatever is the true meaning of the Dharma
Has been realized by no one,
Taught by no one, and known by no one:
So the supreme Sage has said."

The god Understanding the Conventions of the Dharma said:

"Defilement, stain, and affliction,
Habitual tendency, thought, and examination,
Total affliction and complete purification:
O Sage, these are merely conventional words."

The god Skilled in Conceptualization said:

"Total affliction and complete purification,
Defilements and afflictions,
Purity, explanation, kinsfolk,
And Teacher: all these are mere names."

The god Nonabiding Intelligence said:

"Unattached to the three realms,
You whose mind is like space,
Freed, and worthy of offerings,
To the peaceful mind I bow."

The god Unstained said:

"O Sage, your mind
Abides neither in form nor in the formless;
In desire and in nirvāṇa
It does not abide; it is like the sky."

The god Without Hope said:

"Hope for the desire realm abandoned,
In neither the form nor the formless realm,
Freed from all busyness,
You whose craving is exhausted, to you I bow."

The god Craving Uprooted said:

"Toward arising and perishing
Your craving is completely severed;
You have no attachment to any of the three realms.
To the Guide I bow."

The god Overpowering Craving said:

"Having outshone these worlds,
You act as the sublime and supreme bull,
And liberate the sentient beings of the world.
Compassionate one, to you I bow."

The god Free of Craving said:

"Free of the fetters,
Peaceful, having put an end to further existence,
Pure in nature, pure in mind,
To you who are free of time I bow."

The god Knowing Things as They Are said:

"That all phenomena are without entity,
You, O hero, know just as it is.
There is no bondage and no liberation.
To you who see truly I bow."

The god Crossed Beyond the Swamp said:

"You have crossed beyond the swamp of desire,
And likewise beyond the swamp of existence.
You have no views, you have no grasping.
To you whose further existence is exhausted I bow."

The god Material Things Abandoned said:

"You have abandoned material things, abandoned stains,
Abandoned hope, and abandoned evil as well.
Buddha who has wholly abandoned the obscurations,
You who realize the meaning, to you I bow."

The god Hope Abandoned said:

"You have abandoned what is meaningful and what is not;
Without attachment, with certain realization,
You have passed wholly beyond the noose of Māra.
You who are without grasping, to you I bow."

The god Wishing to Benefit said:

"The path, for oneself and for others,
That is free of the fever of the afflictions,
You who see without obscuration,
Great hero, see that as well."

The god Attained Renown said:

"Guide renowned throughout the world,
Those who strive in your wisdom,
If they contemplate just as you have taught,
Will see their afflictions subside."

The god Fearless said:

"Because you are certain about all phenomena,
You see no cause for dismay whatsoever.
Ocean of wisdom, undaunted,
You roar the lion's roar."

The god Fearless of Anything said:

"You have utterly crossed beyond all fears,
And drawn out every thorn.
You have no trepidation and no faintheartedness.
Overpowering all, to you I bow."

The god Abodeless said:

"The gods and the gandharvas,
Together with Māra and Brahmā,
Are unable to challenge your Dharma;
Therefore you have no trepidation."

The god Lion's Prowess said:

"Because you fully know all phenomena,
Having become a buddha, you have no fear.
'Who could challenge me on the Dharma?'
Such a doubt, O Sugata, you do not have."

The god Unafraid said:

"The lion that dwells in mountain ravines
Is without fear and without terror.
Having subdued the small beasts,
Undaunted, he roars in the forest.
Likewise the Omniscient One
Strikes terror into the non-Buddhists (tīrthikas),
And in the world of gods, nāgas, and gandharvas,
Seeing no equal, proclaims his speech."

The god Overpowering All said:

"Guide of the world, in the world
There is no one in the three worlds
Who could make the Buddha himself afraid;
Therefore the Victor is my refuge."

The god Lion Intelligence said:

"Omniscient One, all-seeing,
Fearless with regard to all phenomena,
There is none like you in the world.
To you, wholly undaunted, I bow."

The god Born in Accord said:

"When you proclaimed your buddhahood,
No one here challenged you.
Guide of the world, there is no one
Who can challenge you on the Dharma."

The god Treasury Holder said:

"Proclaiming the lion's roar,
Because the treasury of your Dharma was upheld,
A thousand koṭis of living beings
Have given rise to the mind of supreme awakening."

The god Conduct in Accord said:

"Those whose conduct accords with the Buddha,
Those great heroes, attain the great attainment.
With minds bent toward supreme awakening,
They become supreme persons."

The god Delighting in the Concordant Dharma said:

"Those who hear your teaching
And bend their minds toward supreme awakening
Are great beings in the world;
They become supreme persons."

The god Pure Mind said:

"Having heard the speech of the perfect Buddha,
Unchallenged in all the worlds,
A thousand koṭis of living beings
Have given rise to the mind of supreme awakening."

The god Completely Pure Mindstream said:

"For a long time, Lord of Men,
You trained well in the Buddha's training;
Therefore your mind-stream is pure,
And you possess the thirty-two excellent marks."

The god's son Undefiled Mind said:
"The Buddha, whose defilements are exhausted,
Proclaims the lion's roar;
Since you abide openly in your pledge,
No challenge to this could ever arise."

The god's son Following Suchness said:
"Gods, sons of gods, or humans
Who could speak against your words
Do not exist in the three worlds;
Therefore, Sage, you are endowed with the powers."

The god's son Skilled in the Exhaustion of Defilements said:
"The defilements of the supreme renunciant
Are entirely removed, without remainder;
Here there is no one who challenges you;
Therefore the Buddha is my father."

The god's son Ever Diligent said:
"'Afflictions remain in you':
Anyone who would challenge you so, Lord of Men,
Cannot be found in any way at all;
Therefore the Buddha has no fear."

The god's son Peaceful Mind said:
"Attachment and aversion are pacified,
Delusion, with its habitual tendencies, is pacified,
And you are pacified even of the karma of misconduct;
Therefore, peaceful-minded one, I bow to you."

The god's son Practice of Yoga said:
"When one looks to see by which yoga
Which affliction is removed,
All your knowledge enters into that;
Therefore I bow to you."

The god's son Skilled in Yoga said:
"The collections of yoga
In which the Guide trained are limitless;
Therefore, Omniscient One,
You have removed all afflictions without remainder."

The god's son Yogic Intelligence said:
"Lamp of the world, your mind
Never falls away from yoga;
Therefore all your afflictions,
Together with their habitual tendencies, are completely cleared away."

The god's son Knowing Peace said:
"All afflictions have been pacified,
The habitual tendencies cleared away without remainder;
Fearless one, together with the children of the Victor,
You act without fear in any way."

The god's son Contemplating Exhaustion said:
"Sage, Omniscient One,
You have removed the afflictions together with their habitual tendencies;
Lamp of the world, for you
No equal at all is found."

The god's son Uprooter of Latent Tendencies said:
"You have cleared away habitual tendencies without remainder;
In you there is no stain of fault at all;
Therefore, Buddha, Omniscient One,
You are the sage among the sages of all the gods."

The god's son Abiding at the End said:
"Because the defilements have been abandoned,
Your birth is not a birth leading to rebirth;
Sage, yours is the final birth;
Therefore, Protector, you have no fear."

The god's son Guardian of Measure said:
"Hero, you have abandoned the seeds;
The sprouts of suffering are burnt up;
The creeper of sorrow is withered away;
I bow to the one free of torment."

The god's son Remover of Stakes said:
"Sage, Omniscient One,
You have also removed the stakes of ignorance;
You have purified the Buddha's conduct;
Therefore, Teacher, you have no fear."

The god's son Gone Beyond Existence said:
"You have truly crossed the ocean of existence;
Guide, you have done what was to be done;
You have brought forth the light;
Fearless one, you have accomplished the aim."

The god's son Going to Nirvāṇa said:
"Omniscient One who has become peace,
Who has utterly destroyed all afflictions,
In whom not even a particle of habitual tendency remains,
Protector, I bow to you."

The god's son Banner of Dharma said:
"You raised the banner of the Dharma;
You cast down the banner of pride;
The limitless highway of the Dharma,
Great hero, you have shown."

The god's son Source of Dharma said:
"The Buddha is the source of the Dharma;
Sage who knows all phenomena,
Spiritual friend of all sentient beings,
Ocean of wisdom, I bow to you."

The god's son Robed in Dharma said:
"Though your fearless sons
Proclaim the lion's roar,
Those children of the Victor are never satisfied;
They thirst for the meaning of the Buddha's Dharma."

The god's son Seeking the Dharma said:
"To seek the qualities of the Buddha,
Your sons give rise to the resolve;
Having heard of your fearlessness,
Those wise ones seek awakening."

The god's son Thirsting for Dharma said:
"Having heard of your fearlessness,
In order to seek the qualities of the Buddha,
Ten million living beings became thirsty
And took up steadfast diligence."

The eleventh fascicle.

The god Exalted by Dharma said:

"Having seen you, Buddha, exalted by the Dharma,
Who tame beings fearlessly,
Supreme among all speakers,
Those of supreme discipline seek awakening."

The god Dharma Holder said:

"Sage, the Dharma you have taught
Is profound and hard to see.
When your heirs have heard it,
They seek unsurpassed awakening."

The god Free of Views said:

"Having heard your lion's speech,
Your sons, the heroes,
Without regard for body or life,
Strive for the sake of awakening."

The god Intelligence That Thinks of Nothing Else said:

"Sage free of all timidity,
Having heard this sacred Dharma of yours,
Thinking of nothing else at all,
Those of supreme discipline seek awakening."

The god Not Going Elsewhere said:

"Lord of men, having heard your speech,
They abandon the two vehicles,
And, going by no other path,
They seek unsurpassed awakening."

The god Abiding Near said:

"When the heirs of the Victor hear
This lion's roar you proclaimed,
They draw near to supreme awakening
And become the best of two-legged beings."

The god Eloquence Drawn Near said:

"Having heard the eloquence
With which you, of great renown, have spoken,
All your heirs give rise
To great joy in the qualities of the Buddha."

The god Attained Eloquence said:

"When your blameless heirs hear
What you, fearless one, have proclaimed,
Their eloquence never at any time
Turns back from buddhahood."

The god Ever Joyful said:

"Through what you, fearless one, proclaimed,
Your sons, O one of great strength,
Are ever filled with joy,
And those blameless ones seek awakening."

The god Undaunted Mind said:

"Having heard what you proclaimed,
Your sons, O best of two-legged beings,
Joyful and undaunted,
Seek supreme awakening."

The god Unobstructed Mind said:

"Through hearing what you proclaimed,
Their hearts are gladdened and their minds rejoice;
And never at any time
Are their minds obstructed toward awakening."

The god Skilled in What Does Not Obstruct the Dharma said:

"Whatever dharmas one relies upon
By which the bright side does not wane,
And no obstacle to it arises,
That ground, O Sage, you know."

The god Skilled in Teaching the Dharma Definitively said:

"For one who practices the bright side,
No obstacle will ever arise;
The Sage knows there is no possibility of it.
Therefore the Teacher is free of timidity."

The god Skilled in the Essential Nature of Dharmas said:

"Virtue tainted by affliction
Has the nature of the dark side.
Your mind is not stained by it;
The Sage knows there is no possibility of that."

The god Skilled in Reasoning said:

"That dark dharmas never at any time
Keep company with bright dharmas,
You, Blessed One, have rightly declared.
Therefore, Blessed One, you are my master."

The god Knower of Virtue and Nonvirtue said:

"Great hero, you know all dharmas
Without confusing them with one another:
When one takes up what is afflicted,
One declines from the bright side."

The god Skilled in the Path said:

"When one engages in affliction,
The bright side does not arise;
Not to understand it in this way,
You have said, becomes an obstacle."

The god Desiring Liberation said:

"You have said that relying on the dark side
Obstructs the many
Who long for liberation.
Therefore, Victor, you are omniscient."

The god Pure Mind said:

"Whoever wishes to liberate the mind
Must understand affliction:
So you, great hero, have said.
Thus you are a shrine for the world."

The god Seeing Affliction said:

"If one engages in affliction
Yet does not understand affliction,
One cannot know the sacred Dharma:
So the Sugata has said."

The god Seeing Wrongdoing said:

"Whoever claims that though one indulges in wrongdoing,
Virtue does not diminish,
Has no share in the Dharma of liberation:
So you, best of two-legged beings, have said."

The god To Be Tamed said:

"Whoever wishes to tame attachment,
And whoever wishes to tame aversion and delusion,
Should strive in just the way
The Sage's eloquence sets forth."

The god Purified of Affliction said:

"Those unskilled ones who do not know
Purity and affliction,
For them the Buddha is not their teacher:
So the supreme sage has said."

The god Diligent in Liberation said:

"The Dharma by which afflictions wane,
Which the Victor has taught:
That relying on it, afflictions are not exhausted,
No one can ever raise such an objection against you."

The god Turned Toward Liberation said:

"Regarding the Dharma the Sage taught,
By which all afflictions are exhausted,
The objection that, though one strives rightly, they are not exhausted,
Is not to be found at all."

The god Intent on Yoga said:

"Victor, whatever the purpose
For which you taught the Dharma to the śrāvakas,
That striving for it, one does not attain it:
No one here can raise this objection against you."

The god Fearless Glory said:

"You taught the unattractive
For the sake of abandoning attachment;
That if one strives rightly in it, attachment is not exhausted,
No one can ever raise such an objection against you."

The god Fully Knowing No Going said:

"You who are utterly free of timidity
Taught love for abandoning aversion;
That practicing it does not dispel aversion,
No one can object to you, vanquisher of foes."

The god Pure Eye said:

"Wisdom, which you taught
For the sake of abandoning delusion:
That though one relies on it, delusion is not dispelled,
No one here can raise this objection against you."

The god Destroyer of Conceptuality said:

"As the antidote to conceptual thought
You taught mindfulness of breathing out and in;
That though one strives in it, thought is not stilled,
No one here can raise this objection against you."

The god Devoted to Signlessness said:

"In order to abandon the thought 'I am,'
You taught emptiness;
That it does not bring this to peace,
No one here can raise this objection against you."

The god Who Has Abandoned the Pride of Thinking "I Am" said:

"In order to abandon the pride of thinking 'I,'
You taught signlessness;
That though one relies on it, it does not bring peace,
No one here can raise this objection against you."

The god Pure Mind-Stream said:

"Through purity of superior intention
All afflictions are destroyed;
That by it afflictions are not exhausted,
No one here can raise this objection against you."

The god Dismantler of Perception said:

"In any respect, in the forms of antidote,
In afflictions, the path, or entering the path,
One who could fearlessly dispute with you,
Great Sage, has never been seen."

The devaputra (a son of the gods) Thorough Knower of Conventions said:
"Blessed One, side and no side,
Exhausted and unexhausted, are mere conventions.
The one who disputes without fear,
That sage, is a name and nothing more."

The devaputra Disciplined in Bodily Action said:
"Having fully known through your knowledge,
The activity of your body sets forth.
Because you know all without remainder,
The Guide is therefore the Omniscient One."

The devaputra Fully Knowing Bodily Action said:
"Out of loving care for sentient beings,
Hero, the activity of your body
Is in every way entirely certain.
To the Protector of the World I bow."

The devaputra Utterly Certain in Bodily Action said:
"With the activity of your body wholly certain,
For the benefit of all who have bodies
You act, O lamp of the world;
Therefore you are the supreme person."

The devaputra Clearly Seeing the Limit of Bodily Action said:
"For the benefit of all sentient beings
The Guide, the Omniscient One,
Possesses bodily activity that is pure
And well and thoroughly examined."

The devaputra Well Composed in the Limit of Bodily Action said:
"Possessed of a body perfectly composed
And of bodily activity utterly pure,
For the happiness of sentient beings
The supreme person acts."

The devaputra Accomplished Speech said:
"Speech that is pleasant and full of good qualities,
Speech entirely free of harshness,
Thoroughly examined by your knowledge,
Such, O Protector, is the speech you utter."

The devaputra Speaking at the Right Time said:
"Speech that is timely and meaningful,
Speech entirely free of the pointless,
Fit to be spoken and fit to be accepted,
Such, O Guide, is the speech you utter."

The devaputra Reconciling Intelligence said:
"Shunning entirely what brings division,
Speech that reconciles and pleases,
Whose ripened fruit is freedom from discord,
Such speech you uttered in the past."

The devaputra Undeceiving said:
"Even for the sake of your life
You spoke no lie in any of your existences.
Therefore all your renown
Spreads unhindered through the worlds."

The devaputra Speaking Truly said:
"That all beings in this way,
Seeking the qualities of the Buddha,
Praise the speech of the Sage
Is the result concordant with your speaking truly."

The devaputra Emerged from the Limit of Reality said:
"Supreme among humans, through truth
You accomplished this perfect awakening,
And the true nature of phenomena as well
You know, O best of two-legged beings."

The devaputra Steadfast in Reality said:
"Seeing the phenomena of the conditioned
Correctly, just as they are,
And the true characteristics of phenomena too,
The Lord of Humans fully knows."

The devaputra Examining Action said:
"Through wisdom that is utterly pure
You fully know the actions of speech
Both of yourself and of others;
Therefore, Victor, you have no equal."

The devaputra Examining the Mind said:
"Whatever actions of the mind
Of the Lord of Humans set forth, however many,
They proceed toward beings with compassion.
Benefactor of beings, to you I bow."

The devaputra Skilled in Mental Conceptuality said:
"Having given rise to compassion for beings,
Toward whomever you engage,
Toward them your mind has become gentle.
Benefactor of beings, to you I bow."

The devaputra Skilled in Means said:
"Having examined the dispositions of beings,
The Guide's activity of mind sets forth.
The manifold wisdom of the Sage
Engages everywhere, just as he knows."

The devaputra Dismantler of Mind said:
"The buddha ground and the actions of the mind
Arise from looking on beings with compassion.
In buddhahood itself you fully know
Wisdom and means to be without intrinsic nature."

The devaputra Skilled in the Past said:
"In generosity, moral discipline, patience,
Diligence, and meditative concentration too,
Who practiced them in the past toward whom,
And the former times as well, you fully know."

The devaputra Examining the Past said:
"Seeking supreme awakening,
Wherever you practiced in the past,
Having thoroughly examined that former conduct,
You fully know the buddhas by the ten million."

The devaputra Excellent in Former Ways said:
"For countless ten millions of eons,
Wherever you practiced in the past,
Though you call to mind those former ways,
Your mind meets with no obstruction."

The devaputra Thoroughly Examining Former Ways said:
"The samādhis arisen from many buddhas,
Which are to be brought to full realization,
From countless ten millions of buddhas,
O Guide, you requested."

The devaputra Emerged from Former Application said:
"In the presence of the buddhas of the past,
Doors of samādhi, countless and immeasurable,
The Guide requested
And then meditated upon them."

The devaputra Examining Former Abodes said:
"Seeing the many faults of saṃsāra
And the various kinds of honor,
You teach them to sentient beings,
And you make them plain even to beings who are blind."

The devaputra Conjuring in Former Times said:
"Others and still others ate and drank,
Played and made great merriment,
And did unbearable harm;
These others and others too you behold."

The devaputra Truly Arisen from Former Conduct said:
"Omniscient One, who understands beings,
Your wisdom concerning the past,
Its actions, meditative concentrations and absorptions,
And its beings, meets no obstruction."

The devaputra Skilled in the Later Limit said:
"O Guide, your knowledge
Of the deaths and transmigrations of future times,
Of births, and of the ripening of actions,
Meets with no obstruction at all."

The god Domain of the Future said:

"O wise one, refuge of beings, your knowledge
Of the thoughts of beings in times to come,
Of the fruits of their karma, and of their destinies,
Meets with no obstruction."

The god Moved Beyond Existence said:

"Wise in these three realms
And in all three times,
Your supreme wisdom is unattached;
You know the domain of existence."

The god Engaged in Convention said:

"Karma, sentient beings and their destinies,
And those who experience karmic ripening:
All of these, O supreme among two-footed beings,
You have taught to be mere names."

The god Rightly Examining the Present said:

"Hero, your knowledge
Meets with no obstruction in the present.
The countless buddha fields
The Protector of the World fully knows."

The god Analyzing the Present said:

"Having analyzed, the Lord of Men
Sees the things of the three times,
Which bewilder the minds of the childish,
As nonexistent, and surely as sameness."

The god Nonabiding Intelligence said:

"There is no abiding in the three times,
Nor any abiding in essential nature.
All things are without entity:
So you, the Guide, see."

The god Urging On the Three Times said:

"The aggregates of the past,
Of the future, and of the present,
Do not exist and are not things:
So the Holy Being has taught."

The god Unattached Mind said:

"You have not taught that any being
Of the three times has an essence;
You have taught them to be like illusions,
Mirages, optical illusions, and echoes."

The god Gauging the Three Times said:

"Guide, since your knowledge
Knows all three times entirely
To be like illusions,
Your faculties have no attachment."

The god Knower of Resolve said:

"From the night on which you, hero,
Made awakening manifest,
From that time on, your resolve
Has never diminished at all."

The god Attained Right Resolve said:

"The resolve of the Savior of the World
Is never interrupted in between;
Through that constant resolve of yours,
Your heirs have great resolve."

The god Exalted by Resolve said:

"Just as a lotus rises from the water,
So you are exalted by your resolve;
You are not stained by the worlds,
Just as a lotus is not stained by water."

The god Liberated by Resolve said:

"The Protector of the World and resolve
Are spoken of as the realm of phenomena (dharmadhātu);
Nondual, they are not made into two:
So you, the blameless one, see."

The god Attained Right Diligence said:

"Through diligence, one of great splendor,
You attained perfect awakening.
Although you have done what was to be done, Protector,
You have never given up diligence."

The god Perfect Mindfulness said:

"The mindfulness of the Great Hero
Follows upon all things.
Although you have done what was to be done, Protector,
Your mindfulness never diminishes."

The god Mind in Equipoise said:

"The Protector, ever in meditative equipoise,
Knows the conduct of sentient beings,
And according to their conduct
The Guide teaches beings the Dharma."

The god Devoted to Wisdom said:

"The farther shore of the ocean of wisdom
Of you who have become the lamp of the world
The buddhas could not explain,
Even if they spoke for ten million eons."

The god Skilled in Liberation said:

"Because you have found
Neither liberation nor non-liberation,
In you, the unequaled one,
Liberation never declines."

The god Striving for the Wisdom of Liberation said:

"Sage, since, once liberated,
You know the absence of essential nature,
Your vision of the wisdom of liberation,
Your correct knowledge, never declines."

The god Analyzing Former Existences said:

"From Dīpaṃkara onward,
Hero who sees correctly,
There has been no error in your conduct;
Therefore the buddhas praise you, saying, 'Excellent!'"

The god Engaged in the Profound said:

"Sugata, you have no attachment
Such as other living creatures have.
Since you know the nature of phenomena,
You are not confused about that nature."

The god Vast Intelligence said:

"In you, the Omniscient One,
There is no forgetfulness in any way.
Because the Protector's mindfulness is closely set,
The Sage is like a vessel of sesame oil."

The god Undistracted Mind said:

"In you, the Lamp of the World,
There is no mind that is not in equipoise.
The unshared qualities of a buddha
Are hard for any living creature to understand."

The god Firmly Established in Wisdom said:

"In you, the Sugata,
There is no unexamined equanimity.
The unshared buddha qualities
Of the Lord of Men are inconceivable."

The god All-Overpowering said:

"Bull among men, for you
There is no equal in the three worlds.
Omniscient and undefeated,
You know all phenomena."

The god Grasper of the Essence said:

"Unsurpassed essence of sentient beings,
Fearless with regard to all phenomena,
You who have attained equality with all buddhas,
Buddha, Awakened One, I bow to you."

This was the twenty-fourth chapter, the chapter of the gods of the Pure Abodes.

Then, at that time, other non-Buddhists, sixty thousand ascetics and wandering mendicants, saw the offerings made by the asuras, garuḍas, nāga maidens, nāgas, kumbhāṇḍas, gandharvas, yakṣas, kinnaras, the garland-bearing elephant riders, and the gods of the realm of the Four Great Kings, of the Heaven of the Thirty-Three, of Yāma, of Tuṣita, of Nirmāṇarati, of Vaśavartin, of Brahmā, of Ābhāsvara, of Śubhakṛtsna and of Bṛhatphala. They also heard the prophecies, and they heard the praises spoken by the gods of the realm of the Pure Abodes. They were filled with wonder. Having heard this entry into the way of the Dharma, a way of the Dharma such as they had never heard before, they became of two minds, wavering and doubtful, and they said this to the Blessed One:

"O Gautama, having heard this teaching of the Dharma that we had never heard before, we no longer want the life of wandering ascetics (parivrājakas), nor do we want the life of householders. Having heard this way of Dharma never heard before, we have become hesitant and doubtful about this Dharma, and our minds have not become clear. Even so, ascetic Gautama, we have trust in you. Why is that? Because you, ascetic Gautama, have displayed such a performance of miraculous power. Through your display of that miraculous power, we have seen these vast assemblies of gods, and most of this entire assembly, understanding and rejoicing in what you, Gautama, have so well spoken. That is why we have come to have faith in you, ascetic Gautama.

"O Gautama, the gods of Bṛhatphala said, 'All phenomena are the Tathāgata,' and we have great doubt about this, wondering how all phenomena could be the Tathāgata. We think, 'May the ascetic Gautama teach us the Dharma in such a way that we understand the meaning of what he has so well explained, become free of darkness, and then rise from this seat.' It is with this confidence that we have come to the ascetic Gautama."

When they had said this, the Blessed One spoke to the other non-Buddhists, the caraka ascetics and the wandering ascetics: "Wandering ascetics, therefore I shall question you yourselves. Answer as seems right to you."

"Excellent, ascetic Gautama," they replied, and the wandering ascetics listened to the Blessed One. The Blessed One then said to them, "Wandering ascetics, do you know how entry into the womb takes place?"

They replied, "O Gautama, as we have heard from the treatises, entry into the womb takes place through the coming together of three things. When the father and mother have come together, have become attached, and, having become attached, desire has arisen through their imagining with thoughts of desire, at that time entry into the womb takes place."

The Blessed One said, "Wandering ascetics, what do you think about this? When the father and mother engage in conceptual thought, does that desire arise from the mother's mind?"

They replied, "O Gautama, it does not."

"Does it arise from the mother's conceptual thought?"

"O Gautama, it does not."

"Does it arise from the father's mind?"

"O Gautama, it does not."

"Does it arise from the father's conceptual thought?"

"O Gautama, it does not."

"Wandering ascetics, what do you think about this? Does the father's desire enter the mother's womb?"

"O Gautama, it does not."

"Wandering ascetics, what do you think about this? Does the father's mind enter the mother's womb?"

"O Gautama, it does not."

"Wandering ascetics, what do you think about this? Does the father's imagining enter the mother's womb?"

"O Gautama, it does not."

"Wandering ascetics, what do you think about this? Does the one who dwells in the womb die and pass from among the gods and enter the mother's womb?"

"O Gautama, we do not know."

"Wandering ascetics, what do you think about this? Does the one who dwells in the womb die and pass from among humans, from among asuras, from among hell beings, from the realm of animals, or from the world of Yama, and enter the mother's womb?"

"O Gautama, we do not know."

"Wandering ascetics, what do you think about this? Does the one who dwells in the womb enter the mother's womb as something with form, or as something without form?"

"O Gautama, we do not know."

"Wandering ascetics, what do you think about this? Do form, feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness enter the mother's womb?"

"O Gautama, we do not know."

When they had said this, the Blessed One spoke to those other non-Buddhist wandering ascetics: "Wandering ascetics, this Dharma is profound and subtle. It is to be understood by the wise, by those who know, by the intelligent. It is beyond examination and is not an object of examination. It is hard to see and hard to know. Therefore it cannot be known by those like you, who for a long time have looked elsewhere, been inclined elsewhere, placed your faith elsewhere, striven elsewhere, and dwelt in the ground of other views. And yet, wandering ascetics, sons or daughters of good family who rely on a spiritual friend suited to them will have the eye for the profound dharmas arise.

"Wandering ascetics, it is like this. Suppose a person's eye faculty is impaired. If he relies on a good friend who treats him in a way suited to him, then in this very life his eye will become completely clear, and with that eye he will see forms in this very life. In the same way, wandering ascetics, if a son or daughter of good family whose eye of wisdom is impaired relies on a suitable spiritual friend, the eye of wisdom will become fully purified, and with it they will see the profound dharmas.

"Wandering ascetics, you too have for a long time looked elsewhere. You have been deceived by earlier wrong instructions. You perceive what is not Dharma as Dharma, what is not liberation as liberation, and what is not deliverance as deliverance. You have been ruined by those who are themselves ruined. Wandering ascetics, it is like this. If, for example, a blind man takes a blind man as his guide, it should be understood that both the one who is led and the one who leads will, in that improper way, meet with suffering."

Wandering ascetics, in the same way, some monks or brahmins who are not teachers claim, "We are teachers." Not being perfectly complete buddhas, they claim, "We are perfectly complete buddhas." Not being skilled in the way out, they claim, "We are skilled in the way out." Not having seen the way out, they claim, "We have seen the way out." Not being ford-makers, they claim, "We are ford-makers." Because they claim to be teachers when they are not teachers, whomever they instruct is instructed wrongly. Not being perfectly complete buddhas, whomever they bring to understanding is brought to a wrong understanding. Not being skilled in the way out, whomever they lead out is led out wrongly. Not having seen the way out, to whomever they show the way out it is shown wrongly. Not being ford-makers, whomever they ferry across is ferried across wrongly, and those people meet with suffering for no good reason.

Wandering ascetics, it is like this. Suppose a cowherd who is not skilled in fords drives his cattle across where there is no ford. His cattle, finding neither the far bank nor the near bank, will swirl about and meet with suffering right there, for no good reason. Why is that? Because that cowherd is not skilled in fords. Wandering ascetics, in the same way, when someone who is not a ford-maker claims to be a ford-maker, whoever he ferries across will meet with suffering for no good reason.

Wandering ascetics, I am truly a teacher and claim to be a teacher, so whomever I instruct is instructed correctly. I am truly a perfectly complete buddha and claim to be a perfectly complete buddha, so whomever I bring to understanding is brought to correct understanding. I am truly skilled in the way out and claim to be skilled in the way out, so those whom I lead out are led out correctly. I have truly seen the way out and claim to show the way out, so to whomever I show the way out it is shown correctly. I am a ford-maker and claim to be a ford-maker, so whomever I ferry across is ferried across correctly. Having fully understood the qualities of a teacher, I instruct. Having fully understood the qualities of a buddha, I bring others to understanding. Having fully understood the way out, I lead out. Having seen correctly, I show. Having fully understood the ford, I ferry across, and whomever I ferry across, I ferry across by a safe ford.

So, wandering ascetics, you too long for liberation, and I am a Teacher present before you, one who instructs correctly, who brings to correct understanding, who has seen the way out, a perfect way out, and who is a true ford-maker. Therefore lend your ears, give rise to faith, establish mindfulness, and make your minds eager. In order to attain the qualities not yet attained, to travel a path not traveled before, to go in a direction not gone before, to keep company with companions on the path whose company you have not kept before, to seek a path not sought before, and to obtain a treasury of Dharma not obtained before, take up diligence correctly!

Wandering ascetics, concerning what was said, "Entry into the womb takes place when three things are present," listen well and closely, and keep it in mind. I shall explain to you this Dharma discourse called Entry into the Womb.

Wandering ascetics, what is called "the mother" is taught to be the condition of previously accumulated karma. What is called "the father" is taught to be the cause of previously accumulated karma. What is called "the gandharva" is a synonym for consciousness projected by karma. What is called "the embryo" is the abode and basis of the consciousness projected by karma. What is called "the mother's womb" is the basis of the consciousness projected by karma, for consciousness abiding in the mother's womb is born, grows, and develops.

Wandering ascetics, it is like this. Grasses, trees, medicinal plants, and forests that rest upon the great earth sprout, grow, and develop. In the same way, consciousness abiding in the mother's womb is born, grows, and develops. Having grown, it is born. Once born, it develops further. Having developed, its conduct becomes apparent: from whatever destiny one has died and transmigrated, having been born here or there, one behaves in a manner resembling that destiny. These things are to be known by the wise, not by fools. They are to be known through long association, not in a short time. They are to be known through careful reflection, not by the negligent.

Wandering ascetics, the signs by which the wise can recognize a person who has died and transmigrated from among the hell beings are these. The voice is hoarse. The voice sounds like broken pottery. The speech has to be puzzled out. The voice is loud. The voice is booming. Such a person is always frightened, always trembling, and always has goose bumps. In dreams he sees a heap of fire blazing. He sees mountains moving. He sees embers blazing. He sees beings boiled in cauldrons of iron and copper. He sees men holding clubs. He sees himself being hurled about by rākṣasas. He sees himself impaled alive on a stake. He sees himself overpowered by dogs. He sees himself surrounded by dogs. He sees himself harassed by elephants and snakes. He flees in the four directions and does not know which way to go. He does not trust any being. Wandering ascetics, by these twenty features one should know a person to have died and transmigrated from among the hell beings.

Wanderers, the signs of a person who has passed away from the animal realm and been reborn are these. Such a person is dull and sleeps a great deal. He is very lazy. He eats a great deal. He drags himself along the ground. He has no courage. He speaks indistinctly. He delights in stupidity. He delights in darkness. He delights in mud and mire. Again and again he bites off grass with his teeth. He constantly digs at the ground with his big toe. Again and again flies gather on his head. Again and again he snaps his teeth at the air. He sleeps all curled up. He sleeps on the ground. He is not repelled by filth. Again and again he sniffs at the air. Again and again he eats things before they are ripe. He says one thing and does another, and so is deceitful. He behaves haphazardly. In dreams he dreams that he is smeared with mud. In dreams he dreams that he has gone into a forest and is eating grass. He sees himself surrounded by venomous snakes. He sees himself entering a mountain wilderness. Wanderers, these and other signs, of many kinds, should be recognized as the signs of a person who has passed away from the animal realm. Even if one were to describe them for an eon, or for more than an eon, one could not exhaust them.

Wanderers, the signs of a person who has passed away from the world of Yama are these. His hair is tawny. His eyes are like a fox's eyes. He is always hungry. He is always thirsty. He is very miserly. He has great craving, and does not obtain what he seeks. He sets his hopes on what belongs to others. He is angry. His eyes are red. He craves every kind of food. He is attached to all his possessions, and he does not want to see those who are worthy of gifts. Toward every object he has the thought of stealing it. He delights even in worthless things. He is intent on gain. He delights in impure food and drink. He is jealous of the good fortune of others. He develops craving for the wealth of others. Whatever wealth belongs to others, he thinks of it as "mine." When food is scattered, his mind is displeased. He takes fruit even from the streets of the marketplace. Wanderers, the particular signs of a person who has passed away from the world of Yama should be known to be endless: these and others.

Wanderers, the signs of a person who has passed away from among the asuras are these. He is conceited. He is very quarrelsome. He holds grudges. He is very proud. His body is large. His eyes are large. His eyes are white. His fangs are visible. He is powerful. He has a warlike mind. He delights in sowing discord. He is very proud in all his activities. His teeth are visible. He regards the whole world with contempt. His weapons are sharp. His words are hurtful. He is boastful. He has the strength of ignorance. He has the strength of the afflictions. Wanderers, these and others should be recognized as the signs of a person who has passed away from among the asuras.

Wanderers, the signs of a person who has passed away from among humans are these. He has a sense of wonder. He associates with noble ones. He censures those who are not noble. He does what is virtuous. He desires distinction. He desires fame. He desires renown. He delights in the crafts. He delights in knowledge. His afflictions are slight. He has conscience and a sense of shame. He is perfect in the conduct of a son of good family. He is grateful. He is affectionate toward his friends. He is endowed with generosity. He is discerning. He examines what has been done and what has not been done. He has power of speech. He has the ability to take counsel. He is highly skilled at making peace and at making war. He is capable as a messenger. He has the ability to master every language. He clearly knows what is possible and what is impossible. Wanderers, these and others are the signs of a person who has passed away from among humans.

Wanderers, the signs of a person who has passed away from among the gods are these. He is beautiful. He is very fastidious about cleanliness. He delights in garlands. He delights in perfumes. He delights in incense. He is fond of singing. He delights in ointments. He is not satisfied with paltry sense pleasures, and delights in vast ones. He delights in the sound of every kind of music. He delights in every kind of song. He delights in keeping company with great gatherings of people. He delights in houses, in many-storied mansions, in homes, and in vast wealth. His mind is led by loving-kindness. He has a lighthearted, merry disposition. He speaks gently. He is skilled at giving delight. He delights in ornaments. He has a grand disposition. He is fond of going about. He is fond of exerting his bodily strength. He knows the signs of attachment and the signs of nonattachment. He is skilled at helping others. Wanderers, by these and other signs one should be recognized as a person who has passed away from the heavens.

Wanderers, in order to pass completely beyond these signs, sons or daughters of good family should rely on, attend upon, and serve such spiritual friends as teach the Dharma for the sake of passing completely beyond these signs.

Now, the person who has passed away from the hells, when he was formerly a human being, indulged in aversion, and so he accumulated karma arising from aversion; because of that he was born in the hells. Thus, having found a dwelling in the hells, he took on the conduct of a hell being, and then he is born among humans. On hearing these signs of conduct, he should recognize for himself, "I have passed away from the hells," and in order to abandon the karmic cause of the hells, he should seek a spiritual friend. Those spiritual friends then teach loving-kindness as the antidote to aversion. They teach the factors of awakening endowed with loving-kindness, and the perfection of moral discipline endowed with loving-kindness. Through this he will abandon the latent tendency of aversion, and by cultivating great loving-kindness he will bring all six perfections to completion.

Wanderers, in this regard, consider the person who has died and passed from the animal realm. When formerly a human being, he indulged in delusion, and so he formed karma arising from delusion. Because of that, he was born in the animal realm. Then, having come to dwell in the animal realm, he took on the conduct of the animal realm. So even when he dies and passes from the animal realm and is born among humans, he engages in those very behaviors of the animal realm. From those behaviors he should recognize for himself, "I have passed from the animal realm and been born among humans." In order to transcend those actions of the animal realm completely, he should seek a spiritual friend. Those spiritual friends then teach him dependent origination so that he may abandon delusion, and through this he abandons his latent tendency toward delusion. They also teach the perfection of wisdom, the sameness of the three times, and through this the understanding arises in him that delusion is without intrinsic nature. Or else they teach him the sameness of all phenomena, and through this he perfects the perfection of wisdom and attains omniscience.

Wanderers, in this regard, consider the person who has died and passed from the world of Yama. When formerly a human being, he relied on miserliness, cultivated it, and practiced it often, and so he formed karma arising from attachment bound up with miserliness. Because of that karma, which he committed, accumulated, appropriated, completed, and took on, he was born in the world of Yama. Dwelling in the world of Yama, he took on the conduct of those who live in the world of Yama. So even when he dies and passes from the world of Yama and is born among humans, he engages in those very behaviors of the world of Yama. From those behaviors he should recognize for himself, "I have passed from the world of Yama." In order to transcend those behaviors completely, he should seek a spiritual friend. Those spiritual friends then teach him giving away so that he may abandon miserliness, and through this he abandons his latent tendency toward miserliness. Or else they teach him the factors conducive to awakening that are endowed with giving, and they teach the perfection of generosity. By cultivating it, he perfects the six perfections.

Wanderers, in this regard, consider the person who has died and passed from among the asuras. When formerly a human being, he formed vast roots of virtue, but he also indulged in pride. Because of the karma bound up with pride, which he committed, accumulated, appropriated, completed, and took on, he was born as an asura. Dwelling among the asuras, he took on the conduct of the asuras. So even when he dies and passes from among the asuras and is born among humans, he engages in those very behaviors of the asuras. From those behaviors he should recognize for himself, "I have passed from among the asuras." In order to transcend those behaviors completely, he should seek a spiritual friend. Those spiritual friends then teach him the sense bases, so that he may abandon pride and destroy the pride that thinks "I am," and through this he abandons his pride. Or else they teach him emptiness, and through this he abandons his latent tendency toward pride and the self is destroyed. Or else they teach how whatever comes about arises from a combination of conditions, and through this he abandons his latent tendency toward pride. Or else they teach him the single characteristic of phenomena, and through this he perfects the perfection of wisdom, and by cultivating it he achieves omniscience.

Wanderers, in this regard, consider the person who has died and passed from among humans. When formerly a human being, he relied on the ten virtuous courses of action, cultivated them, and practiced them often. Because of that karma, which he committed, accumulated, appropriated, completed, and took on, he was born as a human being. Dwelling among humans, he took on the conduct of humans. So when he dies and passes from among humans and is born again among humans, he engages in those very behaviors of humans. From those behaviors he should recognize for himself, "I have passed from among humans." In order to transcend those human behaviors completely, he should seek a spiritual friend. Those spiritual friends then teach him the perception of impermanence, and through this he abandons those behaviors together with their latent tendencies. Or else they speak to him in dispraise of saṃsāra and in praise of nirvāṇa, and through this he takes no delight in saṃsāra and strives for nirvāṇa. Or else they teach him the six perfections, and on hearing them he generates the mind set on unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. They also teach him skillful means, and through this he holds those six perfections firmly and quickly attains omniscience.

Wanderers, in this regard, consider the person who has died and passed from the heavens. When formerly a human being, he practiced pure conduct while holding it to be supreme, gave gifts while holding giving to be supreme, and guarded moral discipline while holding it to be supreme. Because of that karma, which he committed, accumulated, appropriated, completed, and took on, he was born among the gods. Dwelling among the gods, he took on the conduct of the gods. So even when he dies and passes from among the gods and is born among humans, he engages in those very behaviors of the gods. From those behaviors he should recognize for himself, "I have passed from among the gods." In order to transcend those behaviors completely, he should seek a spiritual friend. Those spiritual friends then teach him the fault of holding pure conduct to be supreme, and they speak of the qualities of pure conduct that does not abide in anything. They disparage giving that is held to be supreme and praise giving that is free of holding it to be supreme. They censure moral discipline that is held to be supreme and praise moral discipline that is free of holding it to be supreme.

Through this he will abandon his clinging to his own moral discipline and ascetic vows as supreme, together with its latent tendencies. Or else one teaches him the perfection of moral discipline, and by cultivating it he will bring the six perfections to completion. One also teaches him skill in means, and endowed with that he will swiftly go forth to omniscience.

Wanderers (parivrājakas), among these, the person who has died and passed on from the hells should rely on a spiritual friend and listen to the teaching on the sameness of the three times. Having heard it, he should mostly dwell in villages, cities, market towns, provinces, districts, and the precincts of royal palaces, in places where many of the fourfold assembly are present: monks, nuns, laymen, and laywomen. He should dwell there and keep company with them. As he goes about there and follows it through with familiarization, the sameness of the three times, namely that all phenomena have the essential nature of being without entity, will become manifest to him.

Wanderers, among these, the person who has died and passed on from the animal realm should rely on a spiritual friend and, as an antidote to delusion, dwell joyfully together with people skilled in treatises. Through this he will abandon his delusion, and he should correctly examine those treatises and that knowledge of the treatises as to their intrinsic nature. Therefore, for him, the treatises and the knowledge of the treatises are also without intrinsic nature. They will appear to him as "without intrinsic nature," and thereby the sameness of the three times will become manifest to him, and he will swiftly go forth to omniscience.

Wanderers, among these, the person who has died and passed on from the world of Yama, lord of the dead, should attend upon a spiritual friend in reliance on whom the thought of generosity arises in him, through whom the sameness of the three times becomes manifest to him, namely the single characteristic that is the absence of characteristics, and through cultivating which he will swiftly go forth to omniscience. He should attend upon good qualities.

The twelfth fascicle.

Wanderers, among these, the person who has died and passed on from the asuras should attend upon a spiritual friend and do battle with the Māra of the afflictions. That is to say, when one searches correctly, asking, "What is this phenomenon called pride? By what is it grasped? In whom does it arise? Who abandons it?", that pride is not found. That by which it is grasped, that in which it arises, and that which strives to abandon it are not found either. He correctly sees that by grasping it wrongly, oneself and the world are deceived. He correctly sees all phenomena as without intrinsic nature, and he fully understands that a phenomenon which has no intrinsic nature is without entity. He fully understands that, being without entity, it is not established. Being not established, it is unborn, and what is unborn does not cease. He fully understands that what is neither born nor ceases cannot be designated. What cannot be designated is neither past, nor future, nor present. He fully understands that what is not found in the three times is neither attained nor lost.

Wanderers, thus this is the nature of reality of all phenomena. That nature of reality by which all phenomena are suchness, unerring suchness, suchness that is not otherwise, is such that the Tathāgata too is suchness, unerring suchness, suchness that is not otherwise. Therefore all phenomena are the Tathāgata.

Wanderers, I have taught, formerly and now as well, that the designations of the world have pride as their nature. Through pride, dying and passing on from the human state is designated. Through pride, dying and passing on from the heavens is designated. Through pride, dying and passing on, down to the hells, is designated. Where there is no pride, there is no designation. Wanderers, this should be known as the wisdom free of pride. It can be known by one who is endowed with skill in means and with the perfection of wisdom, and who is embraced by a spiritual friend.

Then those sixty thousand wandering ascetics (parivrājakas), having heard this teaching, attained acceptance of the nonarising of phenomena. Having attained acceptance of the nonarising of phenomena, they rose from their seats, bowed their heads at the feet of the Blessed One, and sat down to one side. Having sat down to one side, they then praised the Blessed One in verse:

Protector, through the power of wisdom you are truly exalted;
Among all beings you depend on no other.
You see the conduct of all beings
Like a myrobalan fruit placed in the palm of the hand.

The world is churned up by the many kinds of views,
Like the sky covered over by dense clouds.
Because of them the childish go about deluded,
Like a crowd of blind people who have lost their way.

Some say the world is permanent, the world is impermanent,
Both permanent and impermanent,
Neither permanent nor impermanent;
They are ruined like a lord of elephants tied up in a house.

Some say the world has an end, the world has no end,
Both has an end and has no end,
Neither has an end nor has no end;
They are like, for example, a bird shut in a cage.

Some say that this body is the self;
Some say that this body is other;
Bound up in conceptions, they lie helpless,
Like a bird caught and trapped in a snare.

Some say the world was emanated by Īśvara;
Some declare it arose without a cause.
Entangled all around by the many kinds of views,
They are seen by the Victor as plainly as the moon in the sky.

Just as a foolish bird shut in a cage
Hopes for escape at every hole,
The unskilled are bound by the many kinds of views;
They gain no liberation, like one bound at both feet.

To Brahmā, Prajāpati, and Skanda,
And also to Cakradhara and Vaiśravaṇa,
To the directions and the oceans they all bow down,
Like a thief seized at night by the king.

Like a poor man in debt who, when seized by his creditor,
Pleads for a respite,
The childish who dwell in the many kinds of views
Seek happiness from Īśvara.

Like gangs of thieves consigned to prison,
Beings undergo a hundred sufferings.
You, Tathāgata, see them
As clearly as the five fingers on the palm of your hand.

Lord of men, having brought forth compassion for them,
Knower of beings, you act among beings;
Supreme intellect, you free beings life after life,
Like, for example, a king releasing prisoners.

For the world's benefit, through inconceivable eons,
You performed austerities and attained supreme awakening;
Knowing that sentient beings wander in the many kinds of views,
You set them free from their bonds.

Sugata, lion among humans, foremost of two-legged beings,
Sovereign over the whole collection of dharmas,
You have no delusion about the many kinds of views, which are like illusions;
Sugata, we long to become like you.

The powers by which the Victor gained his strength,
The fearlessnesses by which he dispelled the foes who challenged him,
Roaring the lion's roar in the assembly:
O supreme bull, may we attain these!

That by which the Victor shakes the three worlds,
That by which he sends forth infinite light,
That by which the supreme human gives prophecies to beings:
May we attain that today, in an instant!

Then the Blessed One, understanding the superior intention of those wandering ascetics, smiled. Then the venerable Aśvajit addressed the Blessed One in verse:

You who wish to benefit the world, as for this smile you have made,
Has this assembly of non-Buddhists become greatly exalted?
Supreme human, Self-Arisen One, why have you smiled?
Please declare the cause of that smile.

Seeing that the Sugata has smiled,
O you who wish the world's benefit, these beings are in doubt.
Like beings gazing at the full moon,
They are gazing; Victor, tell the cause of your smile.

Who has made a supreme offering to the Victor?
Who has pleased the Father today?
Who today has beheld the qualities of the Victor?
Wise One, please declare it quickly and well.

On hearing the prophecy concerning the non-Buddhists,
The whole world will greatly rejoice.
Since these have thus been tamed by the Sugata,
You are therefore the guide of all beings.

Victor, please dispel this doubt well;
Please destroy the uncertainty of those who are uncertain.
By that these assemblies will rejoice
And have faith in your intention, O Buddha.

Then the Blessed One replied to the venerable Aśvajit in verse:

Aśvajit, knowing that this was the time,
You did well to question the Victor, victorious over foes.
Because you asked this of the Guide, the Self-Arisen One himself,
Words that benefit the world will also be spoken.

As to how I came to make this smile,
I shall now reveal to you the cause of the smile.
To what I teach, just as it truly is,
Make your mind joyful and listen now!

This assembly of non-Buddhists has been thoroughly tamed;
Abandoning the many kinds of views, they will come to hold the good view.
Seeing the world wandering in the many kinds of views,
They have become compassionate and fully entered upon awakening.

Having completely abandoned the many kinds of views,
They have realized the straight and untroubled view,
And having heard my unobstructed prophecy,
All of them have brought forth the mind set on omniscience.

Having brought forth faith in the Victors of the past,
The compassionate ones, supreme among two-legged beings,

For a full two hundred million Victors,
Seeking unsurpassed awakening, they made offerings and gave service.
To the Victors they offered no small gifts;
They purified discipline and peaceful concentration;
They made the power of wisdom and diligence blaze;
In the presence of the compassionate ones they practiced patience.
Having sought supreme awakening for a long time,
Their minds entered into the six perfections as well.
They put questions to the Victor Victorious over Foes,
And they gladdened the minds of a full hundred buddhas.
Yet through the influence of evil friends,
They had come to dwell in grievous kinds of views.
Having seen the Sugata, the Self-Arisen One,
Through nonattachment they abandoned those kinds of views.
They have rightly understood the Teacher's intent;
Faith abides in them toward the Teacher's intentions.
In the future they will seek awakening,
And they will serve ten million Victors.
In the future eon called Tārakopama,
They will be Victors all of the same name, Destroyer of Pride,
Renowned in every direction with great fame,
Knowers of the World who will set beings free.
Their buddha fields will be perfectly pure,
Their arrays manifold and without equal.
In those buddha fields there will be no one of evil mind;
Only those alike in purity of intellect will dwell there.
That will purify all the lower realms,
And remove the states without leisure. At the end of that time,
Those Victors will possess the strength of long life,
Able to live for eighty thousand years.
The people there, having heard of this awakening,
Will not then act in ways that give displeasure.
If they hear the names of those whose minds are free of attachment,
Even women will obtain a male body.
Thus the Teacher, the Victor who destroyed pride, the great sage,
Proclaimed the prophecy concerning the non-Buddhists (tīrthikas).
When they heard it, the worlds together with their gods
Rejoiced, and strength arose in them.

This concludes the twenty-fifth chapter, the Prophecy concerning the Non-Buddhists.

Then King Śuddhodana and his retinue, having heard the prophecies concerning the asuras, the garuḍas, and so on up to the wandering ascetics, thought, "It is wondrous how the Blessed One, by speaking so excellently, has satisfied all beings." Having thought this, he became entangled again by attachment to his son.

Then the Blessed One, knowing the mind of King Śuddhodana, said to King Śuddhodana, "Great King, I shall teach you the pure conduct, a Dharma that is virtuous in the beginning, virtuous in the middle, and virtuous at the end, excellent in meaning, excellent in wording, unmixed, complete, fully pure, and fully cleansed: namely, the Dharma discourse called The Analysis of the Six Elements. Listen to it well and attentively, and keep it in mind, and I shall explain it."

Saying, "Excellent, Blessed One," King Śuddhodana listened to the Blessed One, and the Blessed One then spoke to him as follows: "Great King, what is the Dharma discourse called The Analysis of the Six Elements? Great King, this person consists of six elements, of six sense bases of contact, and of eighteen mental engagements.

"On what basis was it said, 'This person consists of six elements'? Great King, these are the six elements. What are the six? They are the earth element, the water element, the fire element, the wind element, the space element, and the consciousness element. Great King, these are the six elements, and it was on this basis that I said, 'This person consists of six elements.'

"On what basis was it said, 'This person consists of six sense bases of contact'? Great King, these are the six sense bases of contact. What are the six? The eye as a sense base of contact, for seeing forms; the ear as a sense base of contact, for hearing sounds; the nose as a sense base of contact, for smelling odors; the tongue as a sense base of contact, for tasting flavors; the body as a sense base of contact, for touching tangible objects; and the mind as a sense base of contact, for cognizing mental phenomena. Great King, these are the six sense bases of contact, and it was on this basis that I said, 'This person consists of six sense bases of contact.'

"Great King, on what basis was it said, 'This person consists of eighteen mental engagements'? Great King, these are the eighteen mental engagements. What are the eighteen? Here, when a person sees a form with the eye, he engages with the form in a way that gives rise to mental pleasure, to mental displeasure, or to equanimity. When he hears a sound with the ear, he engages with the sound in a way that gives rise to mental pleasure, to mental displeasure, or to equanimity. When he smells an odor with the nose, he engages with the odor in a way that gives rise to mental pleasure, to mental displeasure, or to equanimity. When he tastes a flavor with the tongue, he engages with the flavor in a way that gives rise to mental pleasure, to mental displeasure, or to equanimity. When he touches a tangible object with the body, he engages with the tangible object in a way that gives rise to mental pleasure, to mental displeasure, or to equanimity. When he cognizes a mental phenomenon with the mind, he engages with the phenomenon in a way that gives rise to mental pleasure, to mental displeasure, or to equanimity.

"Great King, the six engagements accompanied by mental pleasure, the six engagements accompanied by mental displeasure, and the six engagements that give rise to equanimity, when gathered together, make eighteen mental engagements. Great King, these eighteen are the mental engagements, and, Great King, it was on this basis that I said, 'This person consists of eighteen mental engagements.'

"Great King, of these, the earth element is of two kinds: there is the internal and there is the external. Great King, what is the internal earth element? It is whatever, within this body, internally, is hard and solid in nature, belonging to it and appropriated by it.

What is that? It is the hair of the head, body hair, nails, teeth, dirt, grime, skin, flesh, vessels, sinews, bones, marrow, heart, liver, lungs, kidneys, stomach, pleura, intestines, mesentery, bladder, spleen, feces, rectum, brain, and brain membrane. Whatever else within this body, inside it, is hard or of a solid character, belongs to the body and is appropriated, is called the internal earth element.

Great King, what is the external earth element? Whatever outside is hard or of a solid character, does not belong to the body and is not appropriated, is called the external earth element.

Great King, when the internal earth element arises, it does not come from anywhere, and when it ceases, it does not go anywhere to accumulate.

Great King, a woman inwardly imagines, "I am a woman." Having inwardly imagined herself as a woman, she outwardly imagines a man to be a man. Having outwardly imagined a man to be a man, she becomes attached and wishes to unite with the external man. The man too inwardly imagines, "I am a man." Having inwardly imagined himself as a man, he outwardly imagines a woman to be a woman.
